The state security apparatus in the UAE has been accused of human rights abuses including forced disappearance, arbitrary arrests and torture.[^cite_note-237][^cite_note-238][^cite_note-239] In a mass trial in 2013, human rights activist and lawyer, [Salim al-Shehhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salim_al-Shehhi?action=edit\&redlink=1) claimed he was held in solitary confinement and forced to give a confession to crimes he was accused of.[^cite_note-240][^cite_note-241] In 2023, the country held its largest mass trial where defendants were not given fair treatment. Beginning in December 2023, a trial with 84 defendants resulted in 43 people sentenced to life in prison, ten received sentences up to fifteen years for peaceful protests against the government. Those convicted were Bangladeshi individuals in the UAE, protesting their home government, while the UAE claimed the defendants were a part of a terrorist organization. The [trial took place in secrecy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Closed_trial) with no documents or evidence provided in the court.[^cite_note-\:14-142][^cite_note-\:13-226][^cite_note-242] In January 2025, [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) (HRW) published a report highlighting the human rights abuses committed by the UAE in 2024, majorly focusing on the unfair mass trial, where the UAE's use of Federal Penal Code and the Cybercrime Law were used to silence government critics, journalists, dissidents and activists, while restricting their freedom of expression.

A December 2024 report by [European Centre for Democracy and Human Rights (ECDHR)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Centre_for_Democracy_and_Human_Rights?action=edit\&redlink=1) highlights the country's acute issue with unlawful, political persecution and absence of judicial independence. Trials are often held in secrecy and the defendants' lawyers are neglected from accessing case files and court documents. The UAE's 2014 Counter-Terrorism Law is used to enforce travel bans, life imprisonment and even death penalty for peaceful critics of the regime and those managing an organization.[^cite_note-243] The UAE's counterterrorism law represses freedom of expression. The country's judicial system lacks any form of transparency or independence, and international organizations have called on the UN to form an independent committee to review all allegations of torture, incommunicado detention, and unfair trials.[^cite_note-244]

Investigations have found that Qatari men have been abducted by the UAE government and allegedly withheld information about the men's fate from their families.[^cite_note-forced-231][^cite_note-qta-245] Over 4,000 Shia expatriates have been deported from the UAE;[^cite_note-246][^cite_note-247] including Lebanese Shia families for their alleged sympathies for [Hezbollah](13919.md).[^cite_note-248][^cite_note-249][^cite_note-250] In 2013, 94 Emirati activists were held in secret detention centres and put on trial for allegedly attempting to overthrow the government;[^cite_note-251] a relative of a defendant was arrested for tweeting about the trial, and sentenced to 10 months in jail.[^cite_note-252] The latest forced disappearance involves [three sisters from Abu Dhabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disappearance_of_the_Al-Suwaidi_sisters).[^cite_note-Amnesty-253]

As a response to their poor human rights records, the government of the UAE has tried to strengthen relations with a number of western private and public entities through [whitewashing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whitewashing_\(politics\)), to improve their public image.[^cite_note-254] They established a Soft Power Council in 2017 in order to address their international image.[^cite_note-255] The UAE deploys a number of these techniques, including gender-washing, [green-washing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenwashing) and [sports-washing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sportswashing); as evident in hosting major events like [COP28](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COP28), sporting events including [Formula One](10854.md) races,[^cite_note-256] the Abu Dhabi Cup in 2026;[^cite_note-257] or promoting tourism and business to Dubai.[^cite_note-258][^cite_note-259][^cite_note-260] The growing interest in playing a role in the international entertainment industriy is an attempt to scour the country's image of its ongoing human rights violations. They continue to face criticism for its ongoing contribution to climate change and human rights abuses, despite participating in these international events.[^cite_note-261] In 2018, the country hosted the Second Annual World Tolerance Summit, which was protested by a number of NGOs and scholars.[^cite_note-262]

### Migrant workers

Main articles: [Migrant workers in the United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Migrant_workers_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates) and [Migrant workers in the Gulf Cooperation Council region](53251682.md)



Two South Asian [blue-collar workers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue-collar_workers) posing for a picture with the [Burj Khalifa](794957.md) on the background

Migrant workers in the UAE are not allowed to join trade unions or go on strike. Those who strike may risk prison and deportation,[^cite_note-mig-263][^cite_note-264] as seen in 2014 when dozens of workers were deported for striking.[^cite_note-265] The [International Trade Union Confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Trade_Union_Confederation) has called on the [United Nations](31769.md) to investigate evidence that thousands of migrant workers in the UAE are treated as slave labour.[^cite_note-266]

In 2019, an investigation performed by *The Guardian* revealed that thousands of migrant construction workers employed on infrastructure and building projects for the UAE's [Expo 2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expo_2020) exhibition were working in an unsafe environment. Some were even exposed to potentially fatal situations due to cardiovascular issues. Long hours in the sun made them vulnerable to heat strokes.[^cite_note-267]

A report in January 2020 highlighted that the employers in the United Arab Emirates have been exploiting the Indian labour and hiring them on [tourist visas](330265.md), which is easier and cheaper than [work permits](14666962.md). These [migrant workers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Migrant_worker) are left open to [labour abuse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labor_rights), where they also fear reporting exploitation due to their illegal status. Besides, the issue remains unknown as the visit visa data is not maintained in both the UAE and Indian [migration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_migration) and employment records.[^cite_note-268]



Dubai construction workers having a lunch break

In a 22 July 2020 news piece, *Reuters* reported [human rights](13831.md) groups as saying conditions had deteriorated because of the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md). Many migrant workers racked up debt and depended on the help of charities. The report cited salary delays and layoffs as a major risk, in addition to overcrowded living conditions, lack of support and problems linked with healthcare and sick pay. Reuters reported at least 200,000 workers, mostly from India but also from Pakistan, Bangladesh, the Philippines and Nepal, had been repatriated, according to their diplomatic missions.[^cite_note-269]

On 2 May 2020, the Consul General of India in [Dubai](211583.md), Vipul, confirmed that more than 150,000 Indians in the United Arab Emirates registered to be repatriated through the e-registration option provided by Indian consulates in the UAE. According to the figures, 25% of applicants lost their jobs and nearly 15% were stranded in the country due to lockdown. Besides, 50% of the total applicants were from the state of [Kerala](4349459.md), India.[^cite_note-270]

On 9 October 2020, *The Telegraph* reported that many migrant workers were left abandoned, as they lost their jobs amidst the tightening economy due to [COVID-19](63030231.md).[^cite_note-271]

Various human rights organisations have raised serious concerns about the alleged abuse of migrant workers by major contractors organising Expo 2020. UAE's business solution provider German Pavilion is also held accountable for abusing migrant workers.[^cite_note-272]

### Environmental policy

Further information: [Environmental issues in the United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_issues_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates)

Environmental issues in the United Arab Emirates are caused by the [exploitation of natural resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exploitation_of_natural_resources), rapid population growth, and high energy demand. [Climate change](5042951.md) contributes to [UAE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UAE)'s [water scarcity](15380061.md), [drought](37335.md), and [rising sea level](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_level_rise).[^cite_note-273] The country is a leading [oil and gas producer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_and_gas). Its [energy consumption per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_consumption) is around 370 [Gigajoule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gigajoule). The UAE's [carbon dioxide emissions per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_carbon_dioxide_emissions_per_capita) are high, ranking sixth among countries globally.[^cite_note-274] Recently, it made efforts to make itself more sustainable. Those include:[^cite_note-275]

- Setting a target to reduce [GHG emissions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GHG_emissions) by 31% in comparison to business scenario by 2030 and reach [net zero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Net_zero_emissions) by 2050.

- Launching a programme to make the 3 most emissions-intensive sectors, 40% more [energy efficient](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Efficient_energy_use).

- Launching some programmes related to [green building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_building). [Retrofitting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Retrofitting) of 30,000 buildings alone, should cut 1 million tons emissions.

- Promoting [public transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_transport) and more.

According to official sources, in Dubai, "the share of mass transport in people's mobility increased from 6 per cent in 2006 to 20.61 per cent in 2022."[^cite_note-276] Together with the USA the country invested 17 billion dollars in [sustainable agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sustainable_agriculture).[^cite_note-277]

## Economy

|  | The **[neutrality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Neutral_point_of_view) of the style of writing in this section is [disputed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:NPOV_dispute)**. Please do not remove this message until [conditions to do so are met](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:POV#When_to_remove). *(January 2024)*_&#x20;([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Maintenance_template_removal))_ |
| --- | --- |

Main article: [Economy of the United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_the_United_Arab_Emirates)



[Burj Khalifa](794957.md), the [tallest human-made structure in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_buildings_and_structures_in_the_world), located in [Dubai](211583.md)

The United Arab Emirates has developed from a juxtaposition of Bedouin tribes to one of the world's wealthiest states in only about 50 years, boasting one of the highest [GDP (PPP) per capita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(PPP\)_per_capita) figures in the world. Economic growth has been impressive and steady throughout the history of this young confederation of emirates with brief periods of recessions only, e.g. in the global financial and economic crisis years 2008–09, and a couple of more mixed years starting in 2015 and persisting until 2019. Between 2000 and 2018, average real gross domestic product (GDP) growth was at close to 4%.[^cite_note-\:2-278] It is the second largest economy in the [GCC](220950.md) (after [Saudi Arabia](349303.md)),[^cite_note-Economy2013-279] with a nominal gross domestic product (GDP) of US\$414.2 billion, and a real GDP of 392.8 billion constant 2010 USD in 2018.[^cite_note-\:2-278] Since its independence in 1971, the UAE's economy has grown by nearly 231 times to 1.45 trillion AED in 2013. The non-oil trade has grown to 1.2 trillion AED, a growth by around 28 times from 1981 to 2012.[^cite_note-Economy2013-279] Supported by the world's seventh-largest oil reserves and aided by prudent investments coupled with a resolute commitment to economic liberalism and strong governmental oversight, the UAE has witnessed its real GDP increase by more than three times over the past four decades. Presently, the UAE is among the wealthiest countries globally, with GDP per capita nearly 80% higher than the OECD average.[^cite_note-\:2-278]



[Dubai International Financial Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai_International_Financial_Centre)



[World Trade Center Abu Dhabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_Abu_Dhabi)

As impressive as economic growth has been in the UAE, the total population has increased from just around 550,000 in 1975 to close to 10 million in 2018. This growth is mainly due to the influx of foreign workers into the country, making the national population a minority. The UAE features a unique labour market system, in which residence in the UAE is conditional on stringent visa rules. This system is a major advantage in terms of macroeconomic stability, as labour supply adjusts quickly to demand throughout economic business cycles. This allows the government to keep unemployment in the country on a very low level of less than 3%, and it also gives the government more leeway in terms of macroeconomic policies – where other governments often need to make trade-offs between fighting unemployment and fighting inflation.[^cite_note-\:2-278]

Between 2014 and 2018, the accommodation and food, education, information and communication, arts and recreation, and real estate sectors overperformed in terms of growth, whereas the construction, logistics, professional services, public, and oil and gas sectors underperformed.[^cite_note-\:2-278]

As for competitiveness, in June 2024 it was reported that the UAE has moved up three place to the 7th place among the top 10 countries in the IMD World Competitiveness. This ranking is issued by the World Competitiveness Centre of the Institute for Management Development (IMD) in [Switzerland](26748.md).[^cite_note-280]

### Business and finance



[Abu Dhabi](18950756.md) skyline

The UAE is ranked as the 26th best country in the world for doing business by the [Doing Business 2017 Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doing_Business_Report) published by the [World Bank Group](36752.md).[^cite_note-Doing_Business-281] The UAE are in the top ranks of several other global indices, such as the [World Happiness Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Happiness_Report) (WHR) and 30th in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index) in 2025.[^cite_note-282][^cite_note-283] The [Economist Intelligence Unit](15303116.md) (EIU) assigns the UAE rank two regionally in terms of business environment and 22 worldwide. From the 2018 Arab Youth Survey, the UAE emerges as the top Arab country in areas such as living, safety and security, economic opportunities, and starting a business, and as an example for other states to emulate.[^cite_note-\:2-278]

The weaker points remain the level of education across the UAE population, limitations in the financial and labour markets, barriers to trade and some regulations that hinder business dynamism. The major challenge for the country, though, remains translating investments and strong enabling conditions into innovation and creative outputs.[^cite_note-\:2-278]

UAE law does not allow trade unions to exist.[^cite_note-ituc-284] The right to [collective bargaining](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collective_bargaining) and the [right to strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_to_strike) are not recognised, and the Ministry of Labour has the power to force workers to go back to work. Migrant workers who participate in a strike can have their work permits cancelled and be deported.[^cite_note-ituc-284] Consequently, there are very few anti-discrimination laws in relation to labour issues, with Emiratis – and other [GCC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooperation_Council_for_the_Arab_States_of_the_Gulf) Arabs – getting preference in public sector jobs despite lesser credentials than competitors and lower motivation. In fact, just over eighty per cent of Emirati workers hold government posts, with many of the rest taking part in state-owned enterprises such as [Dubai Properties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai_Properties) and the airline [Emirates](142715.md).[^cite_note-285] [Western](21208200.md) states, including the United Kingdom, were also warned by the Emirati Trade Minister, [Thani bin Ahmed Al Zeyoudi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thani_bin_Ahmed_Al_Zeyoudi), to keep politics separate from trade and the economy, as it dilutes the agreements' main objectives. In 2023, Al Zeyoudi indicated that these countries should "tone down" the human and workers' rights provisions in the trade deals, in order to gain greater market access and business opportunities.[^cite_note-286]

The UAE's monetary policy stresses stability and predictability. The [Central Bank of the UAE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Bank_of_the_United_Arab_Emirates) (CBUAE) keeps a peg to the US Dollar (USD) and moves interest rates close to the [Federal Funds Rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_funds_rate).[^cite_note-\:2-278]

According to [Fitch Ratings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fitch_Ratings), the decline in property sector follows risks of progressively worsening the quality of assets in possession with UAE banks, leading the economy to rougher times ahead. Even though as compared to retail and property, UAE banks fared well. The higher US interest rates followed since 2016 – which the UAE currency complies to – have boosted profitability. However, the likelihood of plunging interest rates and increasing provisioning costs on bad loans, point to difficult times ahead for the [economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy).[^cite_note-287]



[Dubai Marina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai_Marina) skyline

Since 2015, economic growth has been more mixed due to a number of factors impacting both demand and supply. In 2017 and 2018, growth has been positive but on a low level of 0.8 and 1.4%, respectively. To support the economy, the government is currently following an expansionary fiscal policy. However, the effects of this policy are partially offset by monetary policy, which has been contractionary. If not for the fiscal stimulus in 2018, the UAE economy would probably have contracted in that year. One of the factors responsible for slower growth has been a credit crunch, which is due to, among other factors, higher interest rates. Government debt has remained on a low level, despite high deficits in a few recent years. Risks related to government debt remain low. Inflation has been picking up in 2017 and 2018. Contributing factors were the introduction of a [value added tax](52177473.md) (VAT) of 5%[^cite_note-288] in 2018 as well as higher commodity prices. Despite the government's expansionary fiscal policy and a growing economy in 2018 and at the beginning of 2019, prices have been dropping in late 2018 and 2019 owing to oversupply in some sectors of importance to consumer prices.[^cite_note-\:2-278]

The UAE has an attractive tax system for companies and wealthy individuals, making it a preferred destination for companies seeking [tax avoidance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax_avoidance). The NGO [Tax Justice Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax_Justice_Network) places them in 2021 in the group of the ten largest [tax havens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax_havens).[^cite_note-289] In 2023, the UAE's legal system fell under international scrutiny, as the [members of the British Parliament](3776360.md) opened an inquiry into how the foreign business executives are treated in the country, in case of accusations of breaking the law.[^cite_note-290]

2024 will be the third consecutive year that the UAE holds first place as the world's leading wealth magnet, as 6,700 wealthy migrants are set to move to the country.[^cite_note-291]

### VAT

The UAE government implemented [value-added tax](52177473.md) (VAT) in the country from 1 January 2018, at a standard rate of 5%.[^cite_note-292] While the government may still adjust the exact arrangement of the VAT, it is not likely that any new taxes will be introduced in the foreseeable future. Additional taxes would destroy one of the UAE's main enticements for businesses to operate in the country and put a heavy burden on the economy.[^cite_note-\:2-278]

### Energy

Main article: [Energy in the United Arab Emirates](32020974.md)

See also: [Solar power in the United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_power_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates)



[Ruwais Refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruwais_Refinery) is the fourth-largest single-site oil refinery in the world and the biggest in the Middle East.

Oil and gas production is an important part of the economy of the UAE. In 2018, the oil and gas sector contributed 26% to overall GDP. The UAE leadership initiated economic diversification efforts even before the oil price crash in the 1980s, resulting in the UAE having the most diversified economy in the Middle East and North Africa (MENA) region at present. Although the oil and gas sector continues to be significant to the UAE economy, these efforts have yielded great resilience during periods of oil price fluctuations and economic turbulence. The introduction of the VAT has provided the government with an additional source of income – approximately 6% of the total revenue in 2018, or 27 billion [United Arab Emirates dirham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Arab_Emirates_dirham) (AED) – affording its fiscal policy more independence from oil- and gas-related revenue, which constitutes about 36% of the total government revenue.

The [Barakah nuclear power plant](39806131.md) is the first on the Arabian peninsula and expected to reduce the carbon footprint of the country.[^cite_note-293]

The UAE has solar generation potential, and its energy policy has shifted due to the declining price of solar. The Dubai Clean Energy Strategy aims to provide 7 per cent of Dubai's energy from clean energy sources by 2020. It will increase this target to 25 per cent by 2030 and 75 per cent by 2050.[^cite_note-294]

In 2023, ADNOC and its CEO Sultan Al Jaber closed at least 20 business deals worth nearly \$100 billion. The state oil firm was alleged of exploiting the UAE's COP28 presidency to pursue oil and gas deals. As per leaked documents, Al Jaber's team targeted 16 nations to lobby firms, delegates or ministers on such deals. ADNOC sought deals with companies from 12 countries, which included 11 of the 16 target nations. Al Jaber and senior ADNOC officials openly discussed deals. COP28 organising teams were excluded from meetings and replaced by ADNOC officials, leaving a closed group that made deals.[^cite_note-295]

### Tourism

Main article: [Tourism in the United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates)



[Palm Jumeirah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palm_Jumeirah) in Dubai



[The Dubai Mall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Dubai_Mall)

Tourism acts as a growth sector for the entire UAE economy. [Dubai](211583.md) is the top tourism destination in the Middle East.[^cite_note-tdb-207] According to the annual MasterCard Global Destination Cities Index, Dubai is the fifth most popular tourism destination in the world.[^cite_note-296] Dubai holds up to 66% share of the UAE's tourism economy, with [Abu Dhabi](18950756.md) having 16% and [Sharjah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharjah) 10%. Dubai welcomed 10 million tourists in 2013.

The UAE has the most advanced and developed infrastructure in the region.[^cite_note-297] Since the 1980s, the UAE has been spending billions of dollars on infrastructure. These developments are particularly evident in the larger emirates of Abu Dhabi and Dubai. The northern emirates are rapidly following suit, providing major incentives for developers of residential and commercial property.[^cite_note-298][^cite_note-Topic:_Tourism_industry_of_the_UAE-299]

The inbound tourism expenditure in the UAE for 2019 accounted for 118.6 per cent share of the outbound tourism expenditure.[^cite_note-Topic:_Tourism_industry_of_the_UAE-299] Since 6 January 2020, tourist visas to the United Arab Emirates are valid for five years.[^cite_note-300] It has been projected that the travel and tourism industry will contribute about 280.6 billion United Arab Emirati dirham to the UAE's GDP by 2028.[^cite_note-Topic:_Tourism_industry_of_the_UAE-299]

The country's major tourist attraction includes the famous [Burj Khalifa](794957.md) in [Dubai](211583.md), the tallest tower in the world;[^cite_note-301] [The World archipelago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_World_\(archipelago\)) and [Palm Jumeirah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palm_Jumeirah) also in Dubai;[^cite_note-302] [Sheikh Zayed Grand Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheikh_Zayed_Grand_Mosque) and [Yas Marina Circuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yas_Marina_Circuit) in [Abu Dhabi](18950756.md); [Al Hajar Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Hajar_Mountains) in [Fujairah](58033142.md). The uniqueness of the country's natural desert life, especially with the [Bedouins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bedouins), also facilitates the country's tourist industry.[^cite_note-303]

The UAE will diversify its tourism sector with the introduction of [casino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casino) gambling. [Wynn Al Marjan Island](78424843.md) in [Ras Al Khaimah](61770159.md) will feature the first casino in the country and will also be the country's first [integrated resort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integrated_resort) when it opens in March 2027.[^cite_note-304][^cite_note-305][^cite_note-306]

[Abu Dhabi](18950756.md) is set to become the home of [Disneyland Abu Dhabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disneyland_Abu_Dhabi), the first Disney theme park in the Middle East.[^cite_note-307]

### Transport

Main article: [Transport in the United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates)

[Dubai International Airport](154742.md) became the [busiest airport in the world by international passenger traffic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World's_busiest_airports_by_international_passenger_traffic) in 2014, overtaking [London Heathrow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heathrow_Airport).[^cite_note-308] [Abu Dhabi International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Dhabi_International_Airport) is the second-largest airport in the UAE. Due to the announced expansion of [Al Maktoum Airport](3260694.md) on 28 April 2024, Dubai International Airport will be shut down once Al Maktoum Airport expansion will be completed.[^cite_note-309]



[E 311](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E_311_road_\(United_Arab_Emirates\)), a major road in the UAE

[Abu Dhabi](18950756.md), [Dubai](211583.md), [Sharjah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharjah), [Ajman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajman), [Umm Al Quwain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umm_Al_Quwain), and [Ras Al Khaimah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emirate_of_Ras_Al_Khaimah) are connected by the [E11 highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E_11_road_\(United_Arab_Emirates\)), which is the longest road in the UAE. In Dubai, in addition to the [Dubai Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai_Metro), The [Dubai Tram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai_Tram) and [Palm Jumeirah Monorail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palm_Jumeirah_Monorail) also connect specific parts of the city. There is also a bus, taxi, abra (traditional boat), and water taxi network run by RTA. [T1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai_Trolley), a double-decker tram system in [Downtown Dubai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Dubai), were operational from 2015 to 2019.

Salik, meaning "open" or "clear", is Dubai's electronic toll collection system that was launched in July 2007 and is part of Dubai's traffic congestion management system. Each time one passes through a Salik tolling point, a toll is deducted from the drivers' prepaid toll account using advanced Radio Frequency Identification (RFID) technology. There are four Salik tolling points placed in strategic locations in Dubai: at [Al Maktoum Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Maktoum_Bridge), [Al Garhoud Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Garhoud_Bridge), and along [Sheikh Zayed Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheikh_Zayed_road) at [Al Safa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Safa) and [Al Barsha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Barsha).[^cite_note-310]



A [Dubai Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai_Metro) train. Dubai Metro is the Arabian peninsula's first [rapid transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapid_transit) system and was the world's longest driverless metro network until 2016.

A 1,200 km (750 mi) [country-wide railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rail_transport_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates) is under construction which will connect all the major cities and ports.[^cite_note-IRJMar12-311] The [Dubai Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai_Metro) is the first urban train network in the [Arabian Peninsula](47858.md).[^cite_note-312]

The major ports of the United Arab Emirates are [Khalifa Port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khalifa_Port), [Zayed Port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zayed_Port), Port [Jebel Ali](1875391.md), [Port Rashid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Rashid), [Port Khalid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Khalid_Sharjah?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Port Saeed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Saeed), and Port Khor Fakkan.[^cite_note-313] The Emirates are increasingly developing their logistics and ports in order to participate in trade between Europe and China or Africa. For this purpose, ports are being rapidly expanded and investments are being made in their technology.

The Emirates have historically been and currently still are part of the Maritime [Silk Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silk_Road) that runs from the Chinese coast to the south via the southern tip of India to [Mombasa](200152.md), from there through the Red Sea via the [Suez Canal](29323.md) to the Mediterranean, there to the Upper Adriatic region and the northern Italian hub of [Trieste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trieste) with its rail connections to [Central Europe](5188.md), Eastern Europe, and the [North Sea](21179.md).[^cite_note-314][^cite_note-315]

### Telecommunications

The United Arab Emirates is served by two [telecommunications](33094374.md) operators, [Etisalat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etisalat) and [Emirates Integrated Telecommunications Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emirates_Integrated_Telecommunications_Company) ("du"). Etisalat operated a [monopoly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monopoly) until du launched mobile services in February 2007.[^cite_note-etisalatmon-316] Internet subscribers were expected to increase from 0.904 million in 2007 to 2.66 million in 2012.[^cite_note-317] The regulator, the [Telecommunications Regulatory Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telecommunications_Regulatory_Authority_\(UAE\)), mandates filtering websites for religious, political, and sexual content.[^cite_note-318]

[5G](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5G) wireless services were installed nationwide in 2019 through a partnership with [Huawei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huawei).[^cite_note-319]

## Demographics

Main article: [Demographics of the United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_the_United_Arab_Emirates)



The skyline of [Dubai](211583.md), the largest city in the United Arab Emirates

According to an estimate by the [World Bank](36752.md), the UAE's population in 2020 was 9,890,400. Immigrants accounted for 88.52% while Emiratis made up the remaining 11.48%.[^cite_note-320] This unique imbalance is due to the country's exceptionally high [net migration rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Net_migration_rate) of 21.71, the [world's highest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_net_migration_rate).[^cite_note-321] UAE citizenship is very difficult to obtain other than by [filiation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filiation) and only granted under very special circumstances.[^cite_note-322]



Residential villas in the [Palm Jumeirah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palm_Jumeirah) palm fronds in [Dubai](211583.md)

The UAE is ethnically diverse. The five most populous nationalities for expatriates in the emirates of Dubai, Sharjah, and Ajman are Indian (25%), Pakistani (12%), Emirati (9%), Bangladeshi (7%), and Filipino (5%).[^cite_note-323] Expatriates from Europe, Australia, and North America make up around 100,000 of the population.[^cite_note-arabianbusiness.com-324][^cite_note-325][^cite_note-326] The rest of the population are from other [Arab states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_World).[^cite_note-WorldFactbook-5][^cite_note-327]

About 88% of the population of the United Arab Emirates is urban.[^cite_note-328] The average [life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_life_expectancy) was 76.7 in 2012, higher than for any other Arab country.[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-uaeinteract2-330] With a male/female [sex ratio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_sex_ratio) of 2.2 males for each female in the total population and 2.75 to 1 for the 15–65 [age group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographic_profile), the UAE's [gender imbalance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_mortality_differentials) is second highest in the world after Qatar.[^cite_note-331]

### Language

[Modern Standard Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_Standard_Arabic) is the national language of the United Arab Emirates. English is the most commonly spoken language,[^cite_note-Siemund-2][^cite_note-visitdubai-3] following [British English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_English) conventions.[^cite_note-332] [Emirati Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emirati_Arabic), a variety of [Gulf Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_Arabic), is spoken natively by Emirati people.[^cite_note-333]

### Religion

Main article: [Religion in the United Arab Emirates](28784667.md)



[Sheikh Zayed Grand Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheikh_Zayed_Grand_Mosque) in Abu Dhabi

[Islam](6037917.md) is the largest and the official [state religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_religion) of the United Arab Emirates. The government follows a policy of tolerance toward other religions and rarely interferes in the religious activities of non-Muslims.[^cite_note-US2022-334]

There are more [Sunni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni) than [Shia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shiite) Muslims in the United Arab Emirates,[^cite_note-335] and 85% of the Emirati population are Sunni Muslims with most of them adhering to the [Maliki school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maliki_school) of [jurisprudence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_jurisprudence).[^cite_note-336][^cite_note-337] The vast majority of the remaining 15% are Shia Muslims, who are concentrated in the [Emirates of Dubai](3509285.md) and [Sharjah](210589.md). Although no official statistics are available for the breakdown between Sunni and Shia Muslims among noncitizen residents, media estimates suggest less than 20% of the noncitizen Muslim population are Shia.[^cite_note-US2022-334] The Sheikh Zayed Grand Mosque in Abu Dhabi is the largest mosque in the country and a major tourist attraction. [Ibadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibadi) is common among Omanis in the UAE, while [Sufi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sufi) influences exist as well.[^cite_note-338]

[Christians](18337522.md) account for 9% of the total population of the United Arab Emirates, according to the 2005 census; estimates in 2010 suggested a figure of 12.6%.[^cite_note-US2022-334] [Roman Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholicism_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates) and [Protestants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates) form significant proportions of the Christian minority. The country has over 52 churches in 2023.[^cite_note-339] Many Christians in the United Arab Emirates are of Asian, African, and European origin, along with fellow Middle Eastern countries such as Lebanon, Syria, and Egypt.[^cite_note-340] The United Arab Emirates forms part of the [Apostolic Vicariate of Southern Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Vicariate_Apostolic_of_Southern_Arabia) and the Vicar Apostolic Bishop [Paul Hinder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Hinder) is based in [Abu Dhabi](18950756.md).[^cite_note-341]

There is a small [Jewish](25955086.md) community in the United Arab Emirates. Before 2023, there was only one known [synagogue](48559.md) in [Dubai](211583.md), which has been open since 2008 and the synagogue also welcomes visitors.[^cite_note-342] Another synagogue, [Moses Ben Maimon Synagogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moses_Ben_Maimon_Synagogue), was completed in 2023 as part of the [Abrahamic Family House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abrahamic_Family_House) complex in [Abu Dhabi](18950756.md). As of 2019, according to Rabbi [Marc Schneier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marc_Schneier) of the [Foundation for Ethnic Understanding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foundation_for_Ethnic_Understanding), it is estimated that there are about 150 families to 3,000 Jews who live and worship freely in the UAE.[^cite_note-Ynt-343]

| Religion |  | Per cent |

| [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates) |  | 76% |
| [Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates) |  | 13% |
| [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates) |  | 7% |

South Asians in the United Arab Emirates constitute the largest ethnic group in the country.[^cite_note-346] Over 2 million Indian migrants (mostly from the southern states of [Kerala](4349459.md), [Andhra Pradesh](2377.md), [Coastal Karnataka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanara), and [Tamil Nadu](29918.md)) are estimated to be living in the UAE.[^cite_note-347] There are currently three Hindu temples in the country. Other religions also exist in the United Arab Emirates, including [Jainism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jainism), [Sikhism](27964.md), [Buddhism](3267529.md), [Judaism](15624.md), [Baháʼís](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith), and [Druze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druze).[^cite_note-religiousfreedom-192]

The UAE Minister of Foreign Affairs and International Co-operation, [Abdullah bin Zayed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdullah_bin_Zayed_Al_Nahyan), announced in 2019 the design and construction plan of the [Abrahamic Family House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abrahamic_Family_House), which will serve as an interfaith complex that houses a [synagogue](48559.md), [mosque](19894.md), and a [church](6325.md) on [Saadiyat Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saadiyat_Island) in [Abu Dhabi](18950756.md).[^cite_note-348]

### Education

Main article: [Education in the United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates)



University City Hall is the largest building located in [University City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharjah_University_City) in [Sharjah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharjah). Graduation ceremonies of [American University of Sharjah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_University_of_Sharjah), [University of Sharjah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Sharjah), and [Higher Colleges of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higher_Colleges_of_Technology) are notably held here.

The [education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education) system through secondary level is administered by the [Ministry of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Education_\(United_Arab_Emirates\)) in all emirates except Abu Dhabi, where it falls under the authority of the [Department of Education and Knowledge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Dhabi_Department_of_Education_and_Knowledge). Public schools are divided into [primary schools](25058.md), [middle schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_school), and [high schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_Schools). The public schools are government-funded and the curriculum is created to match the United Arab Emirates' development goals. The medium of instruction in the public school is Arabic with emphasis on English as a second language. There are also many private schools which are internationally accredited. [Public schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_school_\(government_funded\)) in the country are free for citizens of the UAE, while the fees for private schools vary.

The higher education system is monitored by the Ministry of Higher Education. The ministry also is responsible for admitting students to its [undergraduate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Undergraduate_education) institutions.[^cite_note-349] The adult literacy rate in 2015 was 93.8%.[^cite_note-WorldFactbook-5]

The UAE has shown a strong interest in improving education and research. Enterprises include the establishment of the [CERT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CERT_Group_of_Companies) Research Centres and the [Masdar Institute of Science and Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masdar_Institute_of_Science_and_Technology) and Institute for Enterprise Development.[^cite_note-350] According to the QS Rankings, the top-ranking universities in the country are the [United Arab Emirates University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Arab_Emirates_University) (421–430th worldwide), [Khalifa University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khalifa_University)[^cite_note-351] (441–450th worldwide), the [American University of Sharjah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_University_of_Sharjah) (431–440th), and [University of Sharjah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Sharjah) (551–600th worldwide).[^cite_note-352] United Arab Emirates was ranked 33rd in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index) in 2021, up from 36th in 2019.[^cite_note-auto1-353][^cite_note-354][^cite_note-355][^cite_note-356]

### Health

Main article: [Health in the United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates)

In 2018, the life expectancy at birth in the UAE was 76.96 years.[^cite_note-357] [Cardiovascular disease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardiovascular_disease) is the principal cause of death in the UAE, constituting 28% of total deaths; other major causes are [accidents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accident) and [injuries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Injury), [malignancies](105219.md), and [congenital anomalies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congenital_disorder).[^cite_note-cp-358] According to [World Health Organization data from 2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_Body_Mass_Index_\(BMI\)#WHO_Data_on_Prevalence_of_Obesity_\(BMI_above_30\)_\(2016\)), 34.5% of adults in the UAE are clinically [obese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obesity), with a [body mass index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Body_mass_index) (BMI) score of 30 or more.[^cite_note-359]

In February 2008, the Ministry of Health unveiled a five-year health strategy for the public health sector in the northern emirates, which fall under its purview and which, unlike Abu Dhabi and Dubai, do not have separate healthcare authorities. The strategy focuses on unifying healthcare policy and improving access to healthcare services at reasonable cost, at the same time reducing dependence on overseas treatment. The ministry plans to add three hospitals to the current 14, and 29 primary healthcare centres to the current 86. Nine were scheduled to open in 2008.[^cite_note-360]

The introduction of mandatory [health insurance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_insurance) in Abu Dhabi for [expatriates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expatriate) and their dependents was a major driver in reform of healthcare policy. Abu Dhabi nationals were brought under the scheme in June 2008 and Dubai followed for its government employees. Eventually, under federal law, every Emirati and expatriate in the country will be covered by compulsory health insurance under a unified mandatory scheme.[^cite_note-361] The UAE has benefited from medical tourists from all over the [Cooperation Council for the Arab States of the Gulf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooperation_Council_for_the_Arab_States_of_the_Gulf). The UAE attracts [medical tourists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_tourism) seeking [cosmetic surgery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cosmetic_surgery) and advanced procedures, cardiac and spinal surgery, and dental treatment, as health services have higher standards than other Arab countries in the Persian Gulf.[^cite_note-thenational.ae-362]

## Culture

Main article: [Culture of the United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_the_United_Arab_Emirates)



Women in an Emirati folk dance.

Emirati culture is based on [Arabian culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabian_culture) and has been influenced by the cultures of [Persia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persians), [India](14533.md), and [East Africa](260987.md).[^cite_note-folk-363] [Arabian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_architecture) and [Persian inspired architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_architecture) is part of the expression of the local Emirati identity.[^cite_note-loc-364] Arabian influence on Emirati culture is noticeably visible in traditional Emirati architecture and [folk arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folk_arts).[^cite_note-folk-363] For example, the distinctive wind tower that tops traditional Emirati buildings, the *[barjeel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barjeel)*, has become an identifying mark of Emirati architecture and is attributed to Arabian influence.[^cite_note-folk-363] This influence is derived both from traders who fled the tax regime in Persia in the early 19th century and from Emirati ownership of ports on the Arabian coast, for instance the Al Qassimi port of Lingeh.[^cite_note-365]

Major holidays in the United Arab Emirates include *[Eid al Fitr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eid_ul-Fitr)*, which marks the end of *[Ramadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramadan)*, and [National Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Day_\(United_Arab_Emirates\)) (2 December), which marks the formation of the United Arab Emirates.[^cite_note-366]

Literature and other written works by modern Emiratis are not well known globally because the majority of writers in the country do not publish in English, women are restricted from participating in traditional literary groups, and freedom of expression is suppressed.[^cite_note-367] The earliest known poet in the UAE is [Ibn Majid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibn_Majid), born between 1432 and 1437 in Ras Al-Khaimah. The most famous Emirati writers were Mubarak Al Oqaili (1880–1954), Salem bin Ali al Owais (1887–1959), and Ahmed bin Sulayem (1905–1976). Three other poets from Sharjah, known as the Hirah group, are observed to have been heavily influenced by the Apollo and [Romantic poets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romantic_poetry).[^cite_note-poetry-368] The [Sharjah International Book Fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharjah_International_Book_Fair) is the oldest and largest in the country.

Traditional folk dances, such as [*Al-Ayyala*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Ayyala_dance), are performed by men and women in the UAE during festivals, holidays, and weddings.[^cite_note-369] [Liwa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liwa_\(music\)) is a type of music and dance performed locally, mainly in communities that contain descendants of [Bantu peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bantu_peoples) from the [African Great Lakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African_Great_Lakes) region.[^cite_note-poetry-368]

The [list of museums in the United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_museums_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates) includes some of regional repute, most famously [Sharjah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharjah_\(city\)) with its Heritage District containing 17 museums,[^cite_note-370] which in 1998 was the Cultural Capital of the Arab World.[^cite_note-371] In Dubai, the area of [Al Quoz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Quoz) has attracted a number of art galleries as well as museums such as the [Salsali Private Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salsali_Private_Museum).[^cite_note-372] Abu Dhabi has established a culture district on [Saadiyat Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saadiyat_Island). Six grand projects are planned, including the [Guggenheim Abu Dhabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guggenheim_Abu_Dhabi) and the [Louvre Abu Dhabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louvre_Abu_Dhabi).[^cite_note-373] Dubai also plans to build a [Kunsthal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kunsthal) museum and a district for galleries and artists.[^cite_note-374] There are very few [cinemas of the United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_the_United_Arab_Emirates), and any published material is subject to strict censorship guidelines, despite a repeal of the harshest guidelines in 2021. To view a movie in the country you must be 21 years old.[^cite_note-375]

### Media

Main article: [Mass media in the United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_media_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates)

See also: [List of newspapers in the United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_newspapers_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates)



[Dubai Media City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai_Media_City) is home to diverse news and tech companies.

The UAE's [media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_of_the_United_Arab_Emirates) is annually classified as "not free" in the Freedom of the Press report by [Freedom House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_House).[^cite_note-376] The UAE ranks poorly in the annual [Press Freedom Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Press_Freedom_Index) by [Reporters without Borders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reporters_without_Borders). [Dubai Media City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai_Media_City) is the UAE's main media zone. The UAE is home to some pan-Arab broadcasters, including the [Middle East Broadcasting Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_East_Broadcasting_Centre) and [Orbit Showtime Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orbit_Showtime_Network). In 2007, Sheikh [Mohammed bin Rashid Al Maktoum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_bin_Rashid_Al_Maktoum) decreed that journalists can no longer be prosecuted or imprisoned for reasons relating to their work.[^cite_note-377]

At the same time, the UAE has made it illegal to disseminate online material that can threaten "public order",[^cite_note-378] and hands down prison terms for those who "deride or damage" the reputation of the state and "display contempt" for religion. Journalists who are arrested for violating this law are often brutally beaten by the police.[^cite_note-379]

According to UAE Year Book 2013, there are seven Arabic newspapers and eight English language newspapers, as well as a Tagalog newspaper produced and published in the UAE.

New media, such as [Facebook](7529378.md), [Twitter](9988187.md), [YouTube](3524766.md), and [Instagram](31591547.md) are used widely in the UAE by the government entities and by the public as well.[^cite_note-u.aeb-380] The UAE government avails official social media accounts to communicate with public and hear their needs.[^cite_note-u.aeb-380]

In recent years, there has been a notable surge in digital media consumption in the UAE, driven by the widespread use of platforms like Snapchat and TikTok among the younger population.[^cite_note-381]  Influencers on these platforms play a significant role in shaping trends and promoting various products and services.[^cite_note-ndtv.com-382] The government has also implemented digital initiatives to enhance e-Government services and promote smart city concepts, further demonstrating the UAE's commitment to technological advancements.[^cite_note-ndtv.com-382][^cite_note-383]

### Cuisine

Main article: [Emirati cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emirati_cuisine)



[Arabic coffee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_coffee) with [lugaimat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lugaimat), a traditional Emirati sweet

The traditional food of the Emirates has always been rice, fish, and meat. The people of the United Arab Emirates have adopted most of their foods from other West and South Asian countries including Iran, Saudi Arabia, Oman, Pakistan, and India. Seafood has been the mainstay of the Emirati diet for centuries. Meat and rice are other staple foods, with [lamb and mutton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lamb_and_mutton) preferred to goat and beef. Popular beverages are coffee and tea, which can be complemented with [cardamom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardamom) or [saffron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saffron) to give them a distinctive flavour.[^cite_note-384]

Popular cultural Emirati dishes include *threed*, *machboos*, *khubisa*, *khameer*, and *chabab* bread among others while [lugaimat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lugaimat) is a famous Emirati dessert.[^cite_note-385]

With the influence of western culture, fast food has become very popular among young people, to the extent that campaigns have been held to highlight the dangers of fast food excesses.[^cite_note-386] Alcohol is allowed to be served only in hotel restaurants and bars. All nightclubs are permitted to sell alcohol. Specific supermarkets may sell alcohol, but these products are sold in separate sections. Likewise, pork, which is [haram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haram) (not permitted for Muslims), is sold in separate sections in all major supermarkets. Although alcohol may be consumed, it is illegal to be intoxicated in public or drive a motor vehicle with any trace of alcohol in the blood.[^cite_note-387]

### Sports

Main article: [Sport in the United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates)



The [Yas Marina Circuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yas_Marina_Circuit) in [Abu Dhabi](18950756.md)

[Formula One](10854.md) is particularly popular in the United Arab Emirates, and a Grand Prix is annually held at the [Yas Marina Circuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yas_Marina_Circuit) in [Yas Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yas_Island) in Abu Dhabi. The race takes place in the evening, and was the first ever Grand Prix to start in daylight and finish at night.[^cite_note-388] Other popular sports include [camel racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camel_racing), [falconry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falconry), [endurance riding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endurance_riding), and [tennis](29773.md).[^cite_note-389] The emirate of [Dubai](211583.md) is also home to two major [golf](19568112.md) courses: the Dubai Golf Club and [Emirates Golf Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emirates_Golf_Club).

Aside from the leisure aspect, sports can play an important part in shaping national identity. Falconry, for example, has functioned as a national symbol since its inception in 1971.[^cite_note-Falconry-390] Aside from the national prevalence of falconry, the UAE have played a formative role internationally in the co-ordination of [UNESCO](21786641.md)'s recognition of falconry as [intangible heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intangible_heritage).[^cite_note-Falconry-390]

In the past, child camel jockeys were used, leading to widespread criticism. Eventually, the UAE passed laws banning the use of children for the sport, leading to the prompt removal of almost all child jockeys.[^cite_note-391] Recently robot jockeys have been introduced to overcome the problem of child camel jockeys which was an issue of human rights violations. [Ansar Burney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ansar_Burney) is often praised for the work he has done in this area.[^cite_note-392]

#### Football

Further information: [Football in the United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates)



[Zayed Sports City Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zayed_Sports_City_Stadium) in [Abu Dhabi](18950756.md)

[Football](10568.md) is a popular sport in the UAE. [Al Nasr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Nasr_Dubai_SC), [Al Ain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Ain_Club), [Al Wasl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Wasl_FC), [Sharjah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharjah_FC), [Al Wahda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Wahda_FC), [Shabab Al Ahli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shabab_Al-Ahli_Dubai_FC), [Baniyas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baniyas_Club), [Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emirates_Club), and [Al Jazira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Jazira_Club) are the most popular teams and enjoy the reputation of long-time regional champions. The [United Arab Emirates Football Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Arab_Emirates_Football_Association) was established in 1974 and since then has dedicated its time and effort to promoting the game, organising [youth programmes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Youth_program), and improving the abilities of not only its players, but also the officials and coaches involved with its regional teams.

The [UAE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Arab_Emirates_national_football_team)’s senior men's team qualified for the Italy [FIFA World Cup](11370.md) in [1990](157232.md) (along with [Egypt](1022953.md)) and finished at 24th place. It was the third consecutive World Cup with two Arab nations qualifying, after [Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_national_football_team) and [Algeria](1029528.md) in [1982](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_FIFA_World_Cup), and [Iraq](1039652.md) and Algeria again in [1986](157231.md). The UAE has won the [Gulf Cup Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_Cup_of_Nations) twice: the first cup won in January 2007 held in [Abu Dhabi](18950756.md) and the second in January 2013, held in [Bahrain](18933277.md); they were runners-up four times and placed third four times.[^cite_note-393]

The UAE hosted the [1996 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_AFC_Asian_Cup) and went all the way to the final, where they were defeated by the eventual champions and placed 2nd; subsequently they qualified for the [1997 Confederations Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_FIFA_Confederations_Cup) as Asian champions and finished in 6th place. They also finished 3rd at the [2015 Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_AFC_Asian_Cup) in Australia. In the Olympic level the UAE finished 2nd in [2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_2010_Asian_Games) and 3rd in [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_2018_Asian_Games_%E2%80%93_Men's_tournament) Asian Games, and participated in the [2012 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_2012_Summer_Olympics) at London to finish 15th. In the [Arab Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Arab_Cup) they finished 2nd in [2005](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Arab_Friendly_Tournament?action=edit\&redlink=1) at Switzerland and 3rd in 2025 at Qatar. Other tournaments they Won were the [2005 Kirin Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirin_Cup_Soccer), 2000 [LG Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LG_Cup_\(association_football\)), [2013 OSN Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_OSN_Cup) in Saudi Arabia, and [Friendship Tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friendship_Tournament) which they won twice, placed 2nd twice and placed 3rd once.

Regarding Emirati clubs' international level, Al Ain qualified for the [2018 FIFA Club World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_Club_World_Cup), defeating the champions of Oceania, Africa, and South America to ultimately face European champions Real Madrid in the final and finish as runners-up. Al Ain also qualified for the expanded [2025 FIFA Club World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_FIFA_Club_World_Cup), finishing with three points and being eliminated in the group stage. Al Ain won the [AFC Champions League Elite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFC_Champions_League_Elite) twice, in [2003](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002%E2%80%9303_AFC_Champions_League) and [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023%E2%80%9324_AFC_Champions_League), and finished as runners-up twice, in [2005](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005_AFC_Champions_League) and [2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_AFC_Champions_League), a feat also achieved by Shabab Al Ahli in [2015](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_AFC_Champions_League). They also finished third in [1999](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998%E2%80%9399_Asian_Club_Championship), a position also attained by Al Wasl in [1993](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992%E2%80%9393_Asian_Club_Championship).

In the [AFC Champions League Two](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFC_Champions_League_Two), Al Shaab (now Sharjah) finished as runners-up in [1995](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994%E2%80%9395_Asian_Cup_Winners'_Cup) and won the title 30 years later in [2025](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_AFC_Champions_League_Two_final). Eight editions of the [AGCFF Champions League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AGCFF_Gulf_Club_Champions_League) were won by Emirati clubs, most of whom were Al Shabab Al Arabi (part of Shabab Al Ahli today), finished as runners-up four times and third place twice. Al Ain also finished as runners-up for the 2024 [African–Asian–Pacific Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/African%E2%80%93Asian%E2%80%93Pacific_Cup), part of the [2024 FIFA Intercontinental Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_FIFA_Intercontinental_Cup). The only two editions of the [UAE–Morocco Super Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UAE%E2%80%93Morocco_Super_Cup?action=edit\&redlink=1), which were held in 2015 and 2016, ended with the Emirati clubs winning, and the four [Qatar-UAE Super/Challenge Cups and Shields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UAE%E2%80%93Qatar_Super_Cup_Series?action=edit\&redlink=1) in 2025 ended with all Emirati clubs winning them.

Emirati ownership extends to several professional football clubs outside the United Arab Emirates through [City Football Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Football_Group), a UK-based holding company majority owned by an Abu Dhabi–registered investment vehicle controlled by [Sheikh Mansour bin Zayed Al Nahyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mansour_bin_Zayed_Al_Nahyan). The group holds full or partial ownership of clubs in multiple countries, including [Manchester City FC](165813.md) (England), [New York City FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_City_FC) (United States), [Melbourne City FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melbourne_City_FC) (Australia), [Yokohama F. Marinos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yokohama_F._Marinos) (Japan), Girona FC (Spain), [ESTAC Troyes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ES_Troyes_AC) (France), [Lommel SK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lommel_S.K.) (Belgium), and [Palermo FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palermo_FC) (Italy).

#### Cricket

Further information: [Cricket in the United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cricket_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates) and [List of cricket grounds in the United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cricket_grounds_in_the_United_Arab_Emirates)



[Sheikh Zayed Cricket Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheikh_Zayed_Cricket_Stadium) in Abu Dhabi

[Cricket](25675557.md) is one of the most popular sports in the UAE, largely because of the expatriate population from the [SAARC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Asian_Association_for_Regional_Cooperation) countries, the United Kingdom, and Australia. The headquarters of the [International Cricket Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Cricket_Council) (ICC) have been located in the [Dubai Sports City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai_Sports_City) complex since 2005, including the [ICC Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ICC_Academy) which was established in 2009.[^cite_note-394] There are a number of international cricket venues in the UAE, which are frequently used for international tournaments and "neutral" bilateral series due to the local climate and Dubai's status as a transport hub.

Notable international tournaments hosted by the UAE have included the [2014 Under-19 Cricket World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Under-19_Cricket_World_Cup), the [2021 ICC Men's T20 World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Men's_T20_World_Cup), and three editions of the [Asia Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia_Cup) (1984, 1995, and 2018). Notable grounds include the [Sharjah Cricket Association Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharjah_Cricket_Stadium) in [Sharjah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharjah),[^cite_note-395] [Sheikh Zayed Cricket Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheikh_Zayed_Cricket_Stadium) in [Abu Dhabi](18950756.md), and [Dubai International Cricket Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubai_International_Cricket_Stadium) in Dubai.[^cite_note-396]

The [Emirates Cricket Board](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emirates_Cricket_Board) (ECB) became a member of the ICC in 1990. The [UAE national cricket team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Arab_Emirates_national_cricket_team) has qualified for the [Cricket World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cricket_World_Cup) on two occasions ([1996](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_Cricket_World_Cup) and [2015](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Cricket_World_Cup))[^cite_note-397][^cite_note-398] and the [ICC Men's T20 World Cup](8258172.md) on two occasions ([2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_World_Twenty20) and [2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Men's_T20_World_Cup)). The [national women's team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Arab_Emirates_women's_national_cricket_team) is similarly one of the strongest associate teams in Asia, notably participating in the [2018 ICC Women's World Twenty20 Qualifier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Women's_World_Twenty20_Qualifier).

Following the [2009 attack on the Sri Lanka national cricket team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_attack_on_the_Sri_Lanka_national_cricket_team), the UAE served as the *de facto* home of the [Pakistan national cricket team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan_national_cricket_team) for nearly a decade, as well as hosting the [Pakistan Super League](38199013.md).[^cite_note-399][^cite_note-400] The UAE has also hosted one full edition of [Indian Premier League](13260340.md) (IPL) in 2020 and two partial editions of the Indian Premier League (IPL) in 2014 and 2021.[^cite_note-401]
